    "addedLines": [
      "- claude: Catch-all for any task that doesn't fit a more specific agent. FleetView's default when no agent name is typed. (Tools: *)",
      "- Explore: Fast read-only search agent for locating code. Use it to find files by pattern (eg. \"src/components/**/*.tsx\"), grep for symbols or keywords (eg. \"API endpoints\"), or answer \"where is X defined / which files reference Y.\" Do NOT use it for code review, design-doc auditing, cross-file consistency checks, or open-ended analysis — it reads excerpts rather than whole files and will miss content past its read window. When calling, specify search breadth: \"quick\" for a single targeted lookup, \"medium\" for moderate exploration, or \"very thorough\" to search across multiple locations and naming conventions. (Tools: All tools except Agent, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)",
      "- general-purpose: General-purpose agent for researching complex questions, searching for code, and executing multi-step tasks. When you are searching for a keyword or file and are not confident that you will find the right match in the first few tries use this agent to perform the search for you. (Tools: *)",
      "- Plan: Software architect agent for designing implementation plans. Use this when you need to plan the implementation strategy for a task. Returns step-by-step plans, identifies critical files, and considers architectural trade-offs. (Tools: All tools except Agent, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)",
      "- statusline-setup: Use this agent to configure the user's Claude Code status line setting. (Tools: Read, Edit)"
    ],

{
  "parentUuid": "56a59d77-1411-45ae-addf-b26193f5e31c",

    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

oracle ceiling 的 37% (0.0807/0.218)。CED-F1 Spearman r=0.3235 (p≈0)，相关性显著但中等——说明 CED 不是完美的 proxy 但有信号。Random baseline rel_f1=0.4538 说明 sampling diversity 本身就有 +4.8pp 收益，CED 在此基础上再加 +3.3pp。EDCR 下降 (0.6156 vs SFT 0.7118)，N=8 sampling 引入更多 distractor citations。推理耗时 5.8h (N=8 生成主导)。结论：CED 作为 inference-time reranker 有明确论文价值——零训练成本超越 RSFT，且 oracle ceiling 表明更好的 reward model 还有大幅空间。\"}, Root causes and fixes applied to grpo_trainer.py:\\n\\n(A) Checkpoint: save_strategy was \\\"epoch\\\" → now supports --save_steps/--save_total_limit CLI args, defaults to steps-based saving. Validated: checkpoint-1/2 saved in dry-run.\\n\\n(B) Reward weighting: all 3 rewards (format/f1/ced) were equally summed by TRL → added WeightedRewardFn wrapper + --format_reward_weight/--f1_reward_weight/--ced_reward_weight CLI args. Also added --max_steps, --kl_coef (beta). Now I have a clear picture. Let me map the processes to GPUs:

**GPU 0**: PID 675171 → `d112_rsft_s44_train_bf16` (RSFT training for D112, bf16 rerun) — 27102 MiB
  - Registry says: gpu=cuda:0, pid=675161. Actual process PID is 675171 (child process of 675166). Close enough.

**GPU 1**: PID 633119 → `plan017_14b_sft_eval_r2` (14B SFT eval) — 31532 MiB
  - Registry says: gpu=cuda:1, pid=633106. Process PID 633119 is child of 633111. Match.

**GPU 2**: PID 633203 → `plan017_14b_sft_no_evidence_eval_r2` (14B SFT no-evidence eval) — 31934 MiB
  - Registry says: gpu=cuda:2, pid=633190. Close match.

**GPU 3**: Two processes:
  - PID 679723 → `plan019_1_7b_rsft_no_evidence_eval` (1.7B RSFT no-evidence eval) — 5454 MiB
    - Registry says: gpu=cuda:3, pid=679

713. Close match.
  - PID 683952 → `plan019_8b_sft_s43` (8B SFT s43 training) — 26126 MiB
    - Registry says: gpu=cuda:3, pid=683937. Close match.

I'm noticing GPU 3 is running both a small 1.7B eval and a large 8B training job simultaneously, which adds up to about 31580 MiB on a 32607 MiB card—that's pretty tight. The registry shows the 8B SFT job on GPU 3, but I don't see an explicit CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES restriction in the actual command, so I should verify the output directory to confirm what's really happening.

The 1.7B eval is small enough that it should wrap up soon, which would free up some space. But the real problem I'm seeing is that `eval_4b_rsft_no_evidence.sh` is pointing to the wrong adapter path—it's still referencing the old QLoRA adapter that D116 marked as invalid, when it should be using the newer bf16 version that's currently training. Running this script as-is would generate results based on bad weights.

Beyond that, there are a couple of other issues: the eval scripts for the 4B and 8B models aren't setting CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES, so they'd both try to use cuda:0 and could step on each other. And I'm noticing a path mismatch in the 8B eval script—it's looking for `sft_output_qwen3_8b_bf16_seed43` but the actual training output is going to `sft_output_qwen3_8b_s43`. I need to verify what the training script actually specifies for its output directory.

Let me check the actual 14b eval script paths. The registry entries for the running 14B evals reference `bash scripts/14b/eval_14b_sft.sh` and `bash scripts/14b/eval_14b_sft_no_evidence.sh` but these files don't exist in `/root/autodl-tmp/freige/scripts/14b/`. They may be in `/root/autodl-tmp/scripts/14b/` instead (outside freige).

Wait, looking at the directory listing again:
```
-rwxr-xr-x 1 root root  1423 May 18 21:40 eval_14b.sh


```

This is directly in `/root/autodl-tmp/freige/scripts/`. The 14B eval scripts may have been placed there earlier. But the registry for plan017_14b_sft_eval_r2 says command: `bash scripts/14b/eval_14b_sft.sh`. Let me check if there's a `/root/autodl-tmp/scripts/14b/` directory.

Actually, looking more carefully at the 14b eval that's in the seed_expansion dir:
```
-rwxr-xr-x 1 root root  747 May 19 01:15 eval_14b.sh
```
This is in `scripts/seed_expansion/eval_14b.sh`. There's a mismatch between where the scripts actually are and where the registry is pointing to them.

The worker's task was to prep eval scripts for phase 2, and looking at what they actually created around 20:xx on May 19, I can see several issues. The 4B and 8B eval scripts they generated have bugs — some are pointing to the wrong QLoRA adapter paths, and at least one references an incomplete adapter path that cuts off mid-name.

```
adapter_path_8b_bf16_seed43` vs the actual `sft_output_qwen3_8b_s43`)
```

I'm also noticing there are duplicate eval scripts for the 8B SFT s43 setup with conflicting model paths, which is creating confusion about which one is correct.

Looking at the actual training output directory `/root/autodl-tmp/sft_output_qwen3_8b_s43`, I can see both eval scripts are pointing to wrong paths. The first critical issue I'm finding is that `eval_4b_rsft_no_evidence.sh` is still referencing an old QLoRA adapter that D116 explicitly marked as invalid, when it should be pointing to the newer bf16 version that's currently training.

For the 8B model, there's a path mismatch problem—the training completed at `/root/autodl-tmp/sft_output_qwen3_8b_s43`, but the eval scripts are looking in completely different directories, so they won't locate the adapter when they run. Additionally, the no-evidence eval scripts across all model sizes are missing explicit CUDA device assignments...

Looking at the current GPU allocation, everything's staying within the project's allowed range on cuda:0-3, though GPU 3 is running pretty tight with both the 1.7B eval and 8B training combined at around 31.6GB out of 32.6GB available. The D112 bf16 eval script appears to be pointing to the correct adapter path, but there's an inconsistency where some eval scripts properly parameterize GPU selection while others don't, which could create conflicts if multiple jobs launch simultaneously.

Now I'm checking the remaining scripts — they have the necessary existence checks and follow the D076 protocol, so I can move forward with reporting these findings to the Director.

  "text": "## 审查报告 — worker_code_prep_eval_scripts_phase2\n\n**结论**: WARNING\n\nWorker 准备了 4 个新 eval 脚本（`eval_4b_rsft_no_evidence.sh`, `eval_8b_rsft_no_evidence.sh`, `eval_8b_sft_s43.sh`, `eval_d112_rsft_bf16.sh`）。D112 bf16 eval 脚本正确，但其他脚本存在路径不一致问题。\n\n### 发现\n\n- [severity: warning] **`eval_4b_rsft_no_evidence.sh` 指向已失效的 QLoRA adapter**\n  脚本中 `--model_path /root/autodl-tmp/rsft_output_4b_sft_s44`，这是 D116 明确宣告无效的 QLoRA adapter（\"INVALID: QLoRA confound\"）。正确路径应为 `rsft_output_4b_sft_s44_bf16`（当前训练中，已到 checkpoint-400）。如果直接运行此脚本，会产出无效的 no-evidence eval 结果。\n\n- [severity: warning] **8B SFT s43 eval 脚本路径与训练输出不匹配**\n  训练脚本 `sft_8b_s43.sh` 输出到 `/root/autodl-tmp/sft_output_qwen3_8b_s43`（当前运行中，已到 checkpoint-190）。但 Worker 准备了两个 eval 脚本，路径都对不上：\n  - `eval_8b_sft_s43.sh` → `--sft_adapter /root/autodl-tmp/sft_output_qwen3_8b_bf16_seed43`（不存在）\n  - `eval_sft_8b_s43.sh` → `--model_path /root/autodl-tmp/sft_output_8b_seed43`（也不存在）\n  训练完成后两个 eval 脚本都会因找不到 adapter 而失败。\n\n- [severity: info] **`eval_4b_rsft_no_evidence.sh` 和 `eval_8b_rsft_no_evidence.sh` 未参数化 CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES**\n  相比 `eval_8b_sft_s43.sh` 和 `eval_d112_rsft_bf16.sh` 使用 `${GPU:-${CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES:-0}}`，no-evidence 脚本缺少这一参数化。在多进程并发环境中可能导致 GPU 冲突。\n\n- [severity: info] **`eval_d112_rsft_bf16.sh` 正确**\n  指向正确的 bf16 adapter 路径 `rsft_output_4b_sft_s44_bf16`，有 adapter 存在性检查，使用 D076 协议。\n\n### 建议\n\n1. 修正 `eval_4b_rsft_no_evidence.sh` 的 `--model_path` 为 `rsft_output_4b_sft_s44_bf16`\n2. 修正 8B SFT s43 eval 脚本路径统一为 `sft_output_qwen3_8b_s43`（与训练脚本一致）\n3. 为 no-evidence eval 脚本添加 CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES 参数化\n\n### GPU 合规\n当前 5 个进程均在 cuda:0-3 范围内，合规。GPU 3 两进程（1.7B eval 5.4GB + 8B SFT 26.1GB ≈ 31.6/32.6GB）较紧但可运行。",
  "project": "faith-evidence-ie"
}
